# Coding Environment Codex Guide

This file contains rules that apply across projects under `/home/coding`.
Repository and nested `AGENTS.md` files add narrower instructions and take
precedence within their scope. Inspect the applicable repository guidance and
the current working tree before acting.

## Workspace Organization

- Put experiments, temporary clones, and test artifacts under
  `/home/coding/scratch` or a task-specific temporary directory, not directly
  in `/home/coding`.
- Keep each durable project in its own repository directory.
- Preserve unrelated edits and untracked files. This machine runs multiple
  interactive agents and NEEDLE workers concurrently.
- Before editing, inspect `git status --short` and check for a closer
  `AGENTS.md` in the directory being modified.

## Git and Hosting

- Forgejo (`git.ardenone.com`) is the source of truth. GitHub repositories are
  read-only mirrors populated by Forgejo server-side push mirrors.
- New repos are created by pushing to
  `https://git.ardenone.com/jedarden/<name>.git` (push-to-create). The stored
  token cannot create repos via the API. Push-created repos start **private**;
  flip visibility with the repo-settings API only when the repo is meant to be
  public, and only public repos get a GitHub mirror. See CLAUDE.md "Git
  Hosting" for the full recipe.
- Push only to the configured `origin`. Never create client-side dual-push
  configuration.
- Never force-push, including with `--force-with-lease`.
- Do not discard or rewrite changes that may belong to another agent or worker.
- Work directly on `main`. Do not create feature or fix branches, and do not
  use a PR-per-change flow — commit to `main` and push.
- Stage precise paths. Never use `git add -A`, `git add .`, or `git commit -a`;
  name each file or directory being committed. This machine runs multiple
  agents and NEEDLE workers concurrently, so blanket staging can capture build
  output, stray files, and another worker's in-flight edits. Run
  `git status --short` before staging.
- Use this Git identity when configuration is required:

  ```text
  user.name = jedarden
  user.email = github@jedarden.com
  ```

## Infrastructure Changes

- Kubernetes desired state is managed through GitOps. Make managed cluster
  changes in the appropriate manifests under
  `/home/coding/declarative-config`, commit them, push to Forgejo, and let
  ArgoCD reconcile them.
- Do not mutate ArgoCD-managed resources directly with `kubectl apply`,
  `create`, `delete`, `patch`, `edit`, `annotate`, `scale`, or
  `rollout restart`, including as a temporary workaround.
- Read-only cluster inspection is permitted through the configured proxies.
  A genuinely orphaned object that ArgoCD does not own or recreate may be
  cleaned up only after ownership is verified.
- Never use `:latest` image tags in manifests or workflow templates. Pin a
  version or digest.
- Rackspace Spot persistent storage must explicitly use `sata` or
  `sata-large`, never `ssd` or `ssd-large`.
- GitHub Actions are not the deployment path for these repositories. Use the
  configured Argo Workflows and GitOps release flow.

## Rust Commands

The `cargo` executable on this host is wrapped:

- In a clean Git repository with a remote, `cargo test` may be submitted to
  iad-ci for fmt, Clippy, and test verification.
- With uncommitted changes or no remote, Cargo uses a resource-limited local
  fallback.
- Other Cargo commands run locally under resource limits.
- `cargo nextest run` is not remotely redirected.

Do not assume where a command ran. Report the command, its actual outcome, and
whether verification was targeted or complete. Follow repository-specific CI
instructions when they are stricter.

## NEEDLE Fleet Dispatch

- Do not create per-worker git worktrees to isolate concurrent NEEDLE workers
  on a shared repo. Each repo uses a single shared checkout; that is the
  intended model, not an artifact to work around.
- Worktrees add disk and build-cache duplication without fixing the actual
  failure mode. The real risk is a bead getting claimed and worked twice, not
  a file collision — fix overlap at the bead level (check for an existing
  bead before creating a new one, add blocking dependencies between beads
  that touch the same file/function, keep beads narrowly scoped), not by
  giving workers separate worktrees.

## Beads — bead-rs CLI

The canonical bead CLI as of 2026-08-14 is **`bead`** (bead-rs). `bf`
(bead-forge) and its deprecated `br` alias are retired for active work
tracking on ex44 and lab; do not introduce new uses of either in code,
prompts, scripts, or documentation. Bead-forge's own repo stays alive as an
archived project on GitHub — this does not affect that.

**Repos migrate one at a time. Check which CLI a workspace actually uses
before running any bead command** — its `.needle.yaml` declares
`bead_cli.backend: bead-rs` or `backend: bf`; on disk, `.beads/config.json` +
`.beads/checkpoint/` means bead-rs, `.beads/config.yaml` + a flat
`.beads/issues.jsonl` means bf. Running the wrong CLI against a workspace
does not fail cleanly — it reports a generic SQLite schema error, and
applying that *other* tool's own repair recipe to it silently reinitializes
the store with the wrong schema and destroys the live data. This happened
for real to SEAM on 2026-08-14 (recovered losslessly only because its
checkpoint was current). If a bead command errors with an unfamiliar
schema/column error, stop and confirm the backend before attempting any
repair.

SQLite (`.beads/beads.db`) is the live store. `.beads/checkpoint/`
(git-tracked) is the durable checkpoint; nothing flushes implicitly, so
mutations may exist only in SQLite until an explicit flush:

```bash
bead sync flush-only
```

`bead doctor` is safe by design — read-only by default, and even
`--repair` only performs non-destructive housekeeping; it never rewrites
user data or drops tables. To recover a missing, wrong-schema, or corrupt
`beads.db`, rebuild from the checkpoint (lossless if it was flushed
recently):

```bash
bead init
bead sync import-only --input .beads/checkpoint/forensic.jsonl \
  --restore-into-empty --actor <you>
```

Never run `rm .beads/beads.db && bf sync --import` (or any other bf-shaped
recovery command) against a bead-rs workspace — that is the corruption
above, not a fix for it.

Use `--reason` when closing a bead:

```bash
bead close BEAD_ID --reason "Summary of completed work"
```

Do not close a bead when required work or verification is incomplete.

## Operational Discipline

- Prefer reversible, scoped changes.
- Keep commands noninteractive when they may run under automation.
- Do not expose credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, or secret values in output,
  commits, logs, or bead bodies.
- Verify current CLI behavior instead of copying stale examples from plans or
  research notes.
- Put repository-specific architecture, commands, and validation rules in that
  repository's `AGENTS.md`, not in this global guide.
--- NEEDLE/AGENTS.md ---
# NEEDLE Codex Guide

This file is the primary operating guide for Codex in this repository, both in
interactive sessions and when Codex runs headlessly as a NEEDLE worker. Inspect
the current repository state and the relevant source before relying on older
examples or planning documents.

## Project Overview

NEEDLE (Navigates Every Enqueued Deliverable, Logs Effort) is a Rust worker
binary. It selects and claims work through the repository's configured bead
backend, dispatches a coding agent, records telemetry, and routes every outcome
through an explicit state machine.

The primary backend is bead-rs (`bead`); bead-forge (`bf`) remains supported
for explicitly bound legacy workspaces. Check `.needle.yaml` and the `.beads/`
layout before running either CLI. Do not infer the backend from which binaries
happen to be installed, and never use one backend's repair or import command on
the other backend's database. `br` is retired and must not be introduced in
source, prompts, scripts, tests, or documentation.

## Working Safely

- Inspect `git status --short` before editing. Preserve unrelated user and
  worker changes in a dirty worktree.
- Multiple NEEDLE workers may share the same repository. Re-check files before
  modifying them and avoid broad rewrites that can overwrite concurrent work.
- **Do not create per-worker git worktrees to isolate concurrent workers** —
  a shared checkout is the intended model, not a bug to route around.
  Worktrees add disk and build-cache duplication without addressing the
  actual failure mode, which happens at the bead level: the same bead getting
  claimed and worked twice (confirmed 2026-08-09, commitgraph `cg-l0v0kc` —
  two byte-identical commits at the same second from two concurrent workers).
  The supported backends provide atomic claim operations, so avoid this by
  decomposing and dependency-ordering beads so the same unit of work is never
  independently claimable twice — see the target repo's own `AGENTS.md` for
  repo-specific guidance.
- Do not use destructive Git operations (`reset --hard`, forced checkout,
  forced push) to resolve unrelated changes.
- Forgejo (`git.ardenone.com`) is the authoritative remote. GitHub is a mirror.
  Push only to the configured `origin`, and never use `--force` or
  `--force-with-lease`.
- Keep commands suitable for unattended execution: avoid prompts, pagers, and
  commands that require an interactive terminal.
- Treat `docs/plan/plan.md` as architecture and design history. Verify current
  behavior in source and against the installed CLI before depending on old
  command examples.

## Rust Compatibility

The declared MSRV is Rust 1.75 (`Cargo.toml`). Do not add language features or
dependencies that require a newer compiler without intentionally updating the
MSRV and associated toolchain and CI configuration.

## Code Conventions

- Prefer `Result` and `?` for fallible operations. Do not hide operational
  failures with `unwrap()` or `expect()`. Reserve those calls for tests or a
  clearly documented invariant/unrecoverable initialization condition.
- Fallible public operations should return `Result`; infallible constructors,
  accessors, and pure transformations may return ordinary values.
- Match state and outcome enums exhaustively. Avoid catch-all `_` arms where a
  new variant should force an explicit decision.
- Emit telemetry for every state transition and terminal outcome.
- Preserve error context at process, filesystem, database, and parsing
  boundaries.
- Unit tests belong in `#[cfg(test)]` modules near their implementation.
- Integration and end-to-end tests belong under `tests/`.
- Use `#[tokio::test]` for asynchronous tests; do not introduce
  `tokio_test::block_on`.
- Prefer testing public behavior over implementation details.

## Critical Test Isolation

Any test that spawns the compiled `needle` binary as a real subprocess (for
example, `Command::new(CARGO_BIN_EXE_needle)`) must isolate both `HOME` and the
Explore strand's scan root.

Explore is enabled by default and otherwise scans under the real home
directory. An unisolated test can discover and mutate production bead stores.
This previously created hundreds of phantom beads across real repositories.

At minimum, give the subprocess a temporary home:

```rust
cmd.env("HOME", temp_dir.path());
```

Prefer also configuring `workspace_root` to a temporary fixture directory, or
disable Explore when the behavior under test does not require it. Never point a
test worker at `/home/coding` or another directory containing real projects.

## Verification

- Run `cargo fmt --check` for Rust changes.
- Run targeted tests for the modules or behavior changed.
- Run `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` when practical. Existing
  unrelated warnings must be reported distinctly rather than silently fixed as
  part of an unrelated task.
- On this host, the `cargo` wrapper may offload a clean repository to iad-ci and
  use a resource-limited local fallback for a dirty repository. Do not assume a
  command ran remotely; report what actually ran and its result.
- The authoritative full verification is the `needle-ci` workflow on iad-ci
  after a push to `main`. Do not claim full-suite success from targeted tests.
- If required CI fails, record the failure on the bead, fix it, and do not close
  the bead as successfully completed.

## Bead Workflow

Each bead supplies its own deliverables and acceptance criteria. Complete and
verify the requested repository work before closing it.

This repository is bead-rs-backed. Use:

```bash
bead close BEAD_ID --reason "Summary of what was done"
```

The close flag is `--reason`, not `--body`. When no code change is appropriate,
record the reason with a bead comment or another supported public `bead`
operation rather than creating an empty commit.

SQLite (`.beads/beads.db`) is the live store. `.beads/checkpoint/` is the
git-tracked durable checkpoint, and mutations do not flush it implicitly.

Flush explicitly before committing bead state:

```bash
bead sync flush-only
./scripts/checkpoint-publish.sh stage
```

The staging helper resolves and verifies the generation objects named by both
checkpoint pointers, stages those objects atomically with the pointers, and
removes superseded `objects/gen-*.jsonl` files from the working tree. Install
the tracked pre-commit check once per clone with
`./scripts/install-git-hooks.sh`. Do not use `git add -A` for checkpoint
publication.

`bead doctor` is read-only by default. If a database is missing, wrong-schema,
or corrupt, confirm the backend first and restore an empty native store from
the committed forensic checkpoint with the documented `bead init` plus `bead
sync import-only --restore-into-empty` procedure. Never delete or rebuild a
bead database without explicitly accounting for unflushed work.

## Commits

Use the bead identifier when the work is bead-driven:

```text
feat(needle-XYZ): short description
fix(needle-XYZ): short description
test(needle-XYZ): short description
```

Do not mix unrelated cleanup into the task commit. State which checks were run
and any remaining limitations in the final handoff or bead notes.

## Telemetry Contract

NEEDLE emits OpenTelemetry-compatible telemetry via OTLP. When modifying code that interacts with the telemetry system, maintain these semantic conventions:

### GenAI Attributes

The `agent.dispatch` span uses OpenTelemetry's `gen_ai.*` semantic conventions:

| Attribute | Description |
|-----------|-------------|
| `gen_ai.system` | AI provider (e.g., `anthropic`, `openai`) |
| `gen_ai.request.model` | Model identifier (e.g., `claude-sonnet-4-6`) |
| `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens` | Input token count |
| `gen_ai.usage.output_tokens` | Output token count |

These attributes enable NEEDLE telemetry to integrate with GenAI-focused dashboards (Grafana GenAI app, Langfuse, Honeycomb AI, etc.).

### Resource Attributes

Every exported signal carries these resource attributes:

| Attribute | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| `service.name` | `"needle"` |
| `service.version` | Build version from `CARGO_PKG_VERSION` |
| `service.instance.id` | Worker ID (e.g., `needle-claude-anthropic-sonnet-alpha`) |
| `needle.session_id` | Per-boot random session ID |
| `host.name` | Hostname |
| `process.pid` | Worker PID |

For the complete semantic mapping of NEEDLE events to OpenTelemetry signals, see [`docs/plan/plan.md`](docs/plan/plan.md).

src/bead_store/mod.rs-512-/// Retry wrapper for subprocess spawns with exponential backoff for ETXTBSY (errno 26).
src/bead_store/mod.rs-513-///
src/bead_store/mod.rs-514-/// This variant uses **exponential backoff** with jitter, making it more suitable for
src/bead_store/mod.rs-515-/// high-concurrency scenarios where multiple processes might race for the same binary.
src/bead_store/mod.rs-516-/// Exponential backoff prevents thundering herd problems that linear backoff can cause.
src/bead_store/mod.rs-517-///
src/bead_store/mod.rs-518-/// The backoff formula is: `base_ms * 2^attempt + random_jitter`, where:
src/bead_store/mod.rs-519-/// - `base_ms` is the initial delay (default: 20ms)
src/bead_store/mod.rs-520-/// - `attempt` is the current retry number (0-indexed)
src/bead_store/mod.rs-521-/// - `random_jitter` is ±25% of the calculated delay to prevent synchronization
--
src/bead_store/mod.rs-536-/// # Returns
src/bead_store/mod.rs-537-///
src/bead_store/mod.rs-538-/// * `Ok(T)` - The subprocess output on success
src/bead_store/mod.rs-539-/// * `Err(io::Error)` - The last error if all attempts are exhausted
src/bead_store/mod.rs-540-///
src/bead_store/mod.rs-541-/// # When to use exponential vs linear backoff
src/bead_store/mod.rs-542-///
src/bead_store/mod.rs-543-/// Use exponential backoff when:
src/bead_store/mod.rs-544-/// - Spawning multiple processes concurrently that might race for the same binary
src/bead_store/mod.rs-545-/// - Running in high-concurrency environments (CI, parallel tests)
src/bead_store/mod.rs-546-/// - Dealing with slow filesystems where the race window might be longer
src/bead_store/mod.rs-547-///
src/bead_store/mod.rs:548:/// Use linear backoff (`spawn_with_etxtbsy_retry`) when:
src/bead_store/mod.rs-549-/// - Spawning a single process in isolation
src/bead_store/mod.rs-550-/// - The race window is known to be very short (<50ms)
src/bead_store/mod.rs-551-/// - Minimal latency is more important than thundering herd prevention
src/bead_store/mod.rs-552-///
src/bead_store/mod.rs-553-/// # Example
src/bead_store/mod.rs-554-///

src/bead_store/mod.rs-556-/// use std::path::Path;

src/bead_store/mod.rs-558-///

Any test exec-ing its own freshly written script in that window\ngets ETXTBSY. 11 sites in this file write+chmod+exec a script, so the window is\nhit often once tests run concurrently.\n\nWHY THE SYMPTOM LOOKED UNRELATED. Because\nworkspace and args_file are the same tempdir, the discarded error and the panic\nhave the same underlying cause, but only the misleading one is reported. Replace\neach with .expect(\"...\"), so any future spawn failure names itself instead of\nsurfacing as an unrelated NotFound.\n\nThe two exclude_ids tests (panics at ~2604 and ~2667) follow the same shape and\nshould get the same treatment.\n\nACCEPTANCE\n  - cargo test --lib bead_store::tests -- --test-threads=32, run 40 times,\n    produces 0 failures. Because\nworkspace and args_file are the same tempdir, the discarded error and the panic\nhave the same underlying cause, but only the misleading one is reported. That was\nbased on the earlier wall-clock theory, which bf-37hoj disproves — the failure\nis an exec race, not a timeout, and virtual time would not have fixed it.","external_references":[],"id":"needle-d32859e6","issue_type":"task","manual_blocked":false,"notes":"[migrated labels from bf-2um4a]\nci, tests","priority":1,"profile":"native-v1","revision":2,"schema_ref":"urn:bead-rs:schema:issue:native-v1","title":"Retry ETXTBSY on every bead_store spawn path, and stop discarding spawn errors in tests","updated_at":"2026-08-14T16:11:04.108724974Z"},"record_type":"issue"}

Two parts; part 1 alone\nresolves the CI flake, part 2 prevents the next one from being misdiagnosed.\n\nPART 1 — treat ETXTBSY as transient on every spawn path (the actual fix)\n\nspawn_version_check (~line 68 of src/bead_store/mod.rs) already does this\ncorrectly and its doc comment already describes the race. Generalise that\nhandling; do not invent a new pattern.\n\n  Err(e) if e.raw_os_error() == Some(26) && attempt + 1 < MAX_ATTEMPTS => {\n      tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(20)).await;\n  }\n\nApply at all four unprotected spawn sites:\n  - run_br_in    (~line 567, spawn at ~579)\n  - run_bf_batch (~line 850, spawn at ~864)\n  - run_bf_claim (~line 887, spawn at ~926)\n  - run_bf_in    (~line 1591, spawn at ~1608)\n\nrun_bf_in and run_br_in already own a retry loop for transient lock errors, but\nthe spawn is outside it — cmd.spawn()...? returns immediately. Route the spawn\nerror into that existing loop rather than bolting on a second one: ETXTBSY is\ntransient in exactly the same sense a lock error is. Keep the existing behaviour\nthat genuinely non-transient spawn errors (ENOENT, EACCES) still fail fast and\nare NOT retried — retrying a missing binary five times only slows the failure.\n\nThis is a production fix, not just a test fix. NEEDLE hot-reloads its own binary\nand upgrades bf in place, so a worker spawning bf or needle mid-rewrite hits the\nsame errno and currently fails the whole operation.\n\nPART 2 — stop discarding the error that explains the failure\n\nFour tests do \"let _ = store.<call>().await;\" one line before asserting on a\nfile that call was supposed to produce (~lines 2458, 2506, 2559, 2733). Because\nworkspace and args_file are the same tempdir, the discarded error and the panic\nhave the same underlying cause, but only the misleading one is reported. Replace\neach with .expect(\"...\"), so any future spawn failure names itself instead of\nsurfacing as an unrelated NotFound.\n\nThe two exclude_ids tests (panics at ~2604 and ~2667) follow the same shape and\nshould get the same treatment.\n\nACCEPTANCE\n  - cargo test --lib bead_store::tests -- --test-threads=32, run 40 times,\n    produces 0 failures. Baseline before the fix was 15/40 (37.5 percent).\n  - No test in this file discards a Result whose failure it then asserts on.\n  - A deliberately missing binary still fails immediately, not after 5 retries.\n\nDO NOT bother converting these tests to tokio virtual time (bf-4icmm). That was\nbased on the earlier wall-clock theory, which bf-37hoj disproves — the failure\nis an exec race, not a timeout, and virtual time would not have fixed it.","external_references":[],"id":"needle-d32859e6","issue_type":"task","manual_blocked":false,"notes":"[migrated labels from bf-2um4a]\nci, tests","priority":1,"profile":"native-v1","revision":2,"schema_ref":"urn:bead-rs:schema:issue:native-v1","title":"Retry ETXTBSY on every bead_store spawn path, and stop discarding spawn errors in tests","updated_at":"2026-08-14T16:11:04.108724974Z"},"record_type":"issue"}

271-/// # When to use this helper

273-/// Use this wrapper whenever spawning a subprocess that may have been written to
274-/// disk immediately before execution:

354-
355-/// Retry wrapper for subprocess spawns with exponential backoff for ETXTBSY (errno 26).
356-///
357-/// This variant uses **exponential backoff** with jitter, making it more suitable for
358-/// high-concurrency scenarios where multiple processes might race for the same binary.
359-/// Exponential backoff prevents thundering herd problems that linear backoff can cause.
360-///
361-/// The backoff formula is: `base_ms * 2^attempt + random_jitter`, where:
362-/// - `base_ms` is the initial delay (default: 20ms)
363-/// - `attempt` is the current retry number (0-indexed)
364-/// - `random_jitter` is ±25% of the calculated delay to prevent synchronization
365-///
366-/// Example backoff sequence with base_ms=20:
367-/// - Attempt 1: ~20ms (15-25ms with jitter)
368-/// - Attempt 2: ~40ms (30-50ms with jitter)
369-/// - Attempt 3: ~80ms (60-100ms with jitter)
370-/// - Attempt 4: ~160ms (120-200ms with jitter)
371-/// - Attempt 5: ~320ms (240-400ms with jitter)
372-///
373-/// # Parameters
374-///
375-/// * `spawn_fn` - An async function that attempts to spawn the subprocess
376-/// * `max_attempts` - Maximum number of retry attempts (default: 10)
377-/// * `base_ms` - Base backoff delay in milliseconds (default: 20)
378-///
379-/// # Returns
380-///
381-/// * `Ok(T)` - The subprocess output on success
382-/// * `Err(io::Error)` - The last error if all attempts are exhausted
383-///
384-/// # When to use exponential vs linear backoff
385-///
386-/// Use exponential backoff when:
387-/// - Spawning multiple processes concurrently that might race for the same binary
388-/// - Running in high-concurrency environments (CI, parallel tests)
389-/// - Dealing with slow filesystems where the race window might be longer
390-///
391:/// Use linear backoff (`spawn_with_etxtbsy_retry`) when:
392-/// - Spawning a single process in isolation
393-/// - The race window is known to be very short (<50ms)
394-/// - Minimal latency is more important than thundering herd prevention
395-///
396-/// # Example
397-///
398-/// ```no_run
399-/// use std::path::Path;
400:/// use needle::bead_store::spawn_with_etxtbsy_retry_exponential;
401-///
402-/// # async fn example() -> Result<(), std::io::Error> {
403-/// let binary_path = Path::new("/path/to/freshly-written-binary");
404:/// let output = spawn_with_etxtbsy_retry_exponential(
405-///     || async {
406-///         tokio::process::Command::new(binary_path)
407-///             .arg("--version")
408-///             .output()
409-///             .await
410-///     },
411-///     10,
412-///     20,
413-/// ).await?;
414-/// # Ok(())
415-/// # }
416-/// ```
417:pub async fn spawn_with_etxtbsy_retry_exponential<F, Fut, T>(
418-    spawn_fn: F,
419-    max_attempts: u32,
420-    base_ms: u64,
421-) -> std::io::Result<T>
422-where
423-    F: Fn() -> Fut,
424-    Fut: std::future::Future<Output = std::io::Result<T>>,
425-{
426-    use rand::Rng;
427-
428-    const ETXTBSY_ERRNO: i32 = 26;
429-    const JITTER_PERCENT: f64 = 0.25; // ±25% jitter
430-
431-    let mut last_err = None;
432-    let mut rng = rand::thread_rng();
433-
434-    for attempt in 0..max_attempts {
435-        match spawn_fn().await {
436-            Ok(output) => return Ok(output),
437-            Err(e) if e.raw_os_error() == Some(ETXTBSY_ERRNO) && attempt + 1 < max_attempts => {
438-                last_err = Some(e);

use needle::bead_store::{builtin_bead_backends, BeadStore, CliBeadStore};
use needle::types::{BeadId, ClaimResult};
use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::fs;
use std::path::Path;

#[cfg(unix)]
fn executable(path: &Path, body: &str) {
    use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
    fs::write(path, body).unwrap();
    let mut permissions = fs::metadata(path).unwrap().permissions();
    permissions.set_mode(0o755);
    fs::set_permissions(path, permissions).unwrap();
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn store(root: &Path, backend_name: &str) -> CliBeadStore {
    let backend = builtin_bead_backends()
        .into_iter()
        .find(|backend| backend.name == backend_name)
        .unwrap();
    let binary = root.join("fixture-cli");
    executable(
        &binary,
        "#!/bin/sh\nprintf '%s\\n' \"$@\" >> invocations.log\nprintf '%s\\n' '[{\"id\":\"fixture-1\",\"title\":\"fixture\",\"description\":null,\"notes\":\"backend note\",\"priority\":2,\"status\":\"open\",\"assignee\":null,\"labels\":[],\"source_repo\":\"\",\"dependencies\":[],\"dependents\":[],\"comments\":[],\"created_at\":\"2026-08-12T00:00:00Z\",\"updated_at\":\"2026-08-12T00:00:00Z\"}]'\n",
    );
    CliBeadStore::new(backend, binary, root.to_path_buf(), None, None, None).unwrap()
}

#[test]
#[cfg(unix)]
fn descriptor_and_binary_are_bound_together() {
    let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let store = store(root.path(), "bead-rs");
    assert_eq!(store.backend().name, "bead-rs");
    assert_eq!(store.binary(), root.path().join("fixture-cli"));
    assert_eq!(store.workspace(), root.path());
}

#[test]
#[cfg(unix)]
fn rendering_preserves_dialect_specific_dependency_orientation() {
    let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let values = HashMap::from([
        ("blocked", "blocked-1".to_string()),
        ("blocker", "blocker-1".to_string()),
    ]);

    assert_eq!(
        store(root.path(), "bead-rs")
            .render_operation("dep_add", &values)
            .unwrap(),
        ["dep", "add", "blocked-1", "blocker-1", "--kind", "blocks"]
    );
    assert_eq!(
        store(root.path(), "bead-forge")
            .render_operation("dep_add", &values)
            .unwrap(),
        ["dep", "add", "blocker-1", "--blocks", "blocked-1"]
    );
}

#[test]
#[cfg(unix)]
fn absent_optional_velocity_values_remove_their_flags() {
    let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let values = HashMap::from([("actor", "worker".to_string())]);
    assert_eq!(
        store(root.path(), "bead-forge")
            .render_operation("claim_auto", &values)
            .unwrap(),
        ["claim", "--assignee", "worker", "--json"]
    );
}

#[tokio::test]
#[cfg(unix)]
async fn operation_execution_uses_bound_binary_and_workspace() {
    let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let store = store(root.path(), "bead-rs");
    let output = store
        .run_operation("show", &HashMap::from([("id", "fixture-1".to_string())]))
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let beads = store.parse_beads("show", &output).unwrap();

    assert_eq!(beads.len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(beads[0].id.as_ref(), "fixture-1");
    assert_eq!(
        fs::read_to_string(root.path().join("invocations.log")).unwrap(),
        "show\nfixture-1\n--json\n"
    );
}

#[test]
#[cfg(unix)]

    let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();

    let error = store
        .render_operation("show", &HashMap::new())
        .unwrap_err()

    assert!(!root.path().join("invocations.log").exists());
}

#[test]
#[cfg(unix)]
fn bead_forge_list_shape_matches_installed_json_lines_contract() {
    let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let record = |id: &str| {
        format!(
            r#"{{"id":"{id}","title":"fixture","description":"","priority":2,"status":"open","assignee":null,"labels":[],"source_repo":".","dependencies":[],"created_at":"2026-08-12T00:00:00Z","updated_at":"2026-08-12T00:00:00Z"}}"#
        )
    };
    let output = format!("{}\n{}\n", record("bf-a"), record("bf-b"));
    let beads = store(root.path(), "bead-forge")
        .parse_beads("list_all", &output)
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(beads.len(), 2);
    assert_eq!(beads[0].id, BeadId::from("bf-a"));
    assert_eq!(beads[1].id, BeadId::from("bf-b"));
}

#[tokio::test]
#[cfg(unix)]
async fn explicit_bead_rs_claim_uses_revision_guard() {
    let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let backend = builtin_bead_backends()
        .into_iter()
        .find(|backend| backend.name == "bead-rs")
        .unwrap();
    let binary = root.path().join("fixture-cli");
    executable(
        &binary,
        "#!/bin/sh\nprintf '%s\\n' \"$@\" >> invocations.log\nif [ \"$1\" = show ]; then\n  printf '%s\\n' '[{\"id\":\"fixture-1\",\"title\":\"fixture\",\"description\":null,\"priority\":2,\"status\":\"open\",\"assignee\":null,\"labels\":[],\"source_repo\":\"\",\"dependencies\":[],\"dependents\":[],\"comments\":[],\"created_at\":\"2026-08-12T00:00:00Z\",\"updated_at\":\"2026-08-12T00:00:00Z\",\"revision\":7}]'\nfi\n",
    );
    let store =
        CliBeadStore::new(backend, binary, root.path().to_path_buf(), None, None, None).unwrap();

    let result = store
        .claim(&BeadId::from("fixture-1"), "worker-a")
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert!(matches!(result, ClaimResult::Claimed(_)));
    let invocations = fs::read_to_string(root.path().join("invocations.log")).unwrap();
    assert!(invocations.contains(
        "update\nfixture-1\n--status\nin_progress\n--assignee\nworker-a\n--if-revision\n7\n"
    ));
}

#[tokio::test]
#[cfg(unix)]
async fn bead_forge_explicit_claim_uses_atomic_batch_update() {
    let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let backend = builtin_bead_backends()
        .into_iter()
        .find(|backend| backend.name == "bead-forge")
        .unwrap();
    let binary = root.path().join("fixture-cli");
    executable(
        &binary,
        r#"#!/bin/sh
printf '%s\n' "$@" >> invocations.log
if [ "$1" = show ]; then
  if [ -f claimed ]; then
    printf '%s\n' '[{"id":"bf-1","title":"fixture","description":null,"priority":2,"status":"in_progress","assignee":"worker-a","labels":[],"source_repo":"","dependencies":[],"dependents":[],"comments":[],"created_at":"2026-08-12T00:00:00Z","updated_at":"2026-08-12T00:00:00Z"}]'
  else
    printf '%s\n' '[{"id":"bf-1","title":"fixture","description":null,"priority":2,"status":"open","assignee":null,"labels":[],"source_repo":"","dependencies":[],"dependents":[],"comments":[],"created_at":"2026-08-12T00:00:00Z","updated_at":"2026-08-12T00:00:00Z"}]'
  fi
elif [ "$1" = batch ]; then
  touch claimed
fi
"#,
    );
    let store =
        CliBeadStore::new(backend, binary, root.path().to_path_buf(), None, None, None).unwrap();

    let result = store
        .claim(&BeadId::from("bf-1"), "worker-a")
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert!(matches!(result, ClaimResult::Claimed(_)));
    let invocations = fs::read_to_string(root.path().join("invocations.log")).unwrap();
    assert!(invocations.contains("batch\n--json\n"));
    assert!(invocations
        .contains(r#"[{"assignee":"worker-a","id":"bf-1","op":"update","status":"in_progress"}]"#));
    assert!(!invocations.contains("update\nbf-1\n--assignee\n"));
}

#[tokio::test]
#[cfg(unix)]
async fn bead_forge_split_is_one_transactional_batch() {
    use needle::bead_store::NewChild;

    let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let backend = builtin_bead_backends()
        .into_iter()
        .find(|backend| backend.name == "bead-forge")
        .unwrap();
    let binary = root.path().join("fixture-cli");
    executable(
        &binary,
        "#!/bin/sh\nprintf '%s\\n' \"$@\" >> invocations.log\nprintf '%s\\n' '[op 0] ok: bf-child-a' '[op 1] ok: bf-child-b' '[op 2] ok' '[op 3] ok'\n",
    );
    let store =
        CliBeadStore::new(backend, binary, root.path().to_path_buf(), None, None, None).unwrap();
    let labels_a = ["one"];
    let labels_b = ["two"];
    let children = [
        NewChild {
            title: "A",
            body: "body A",
            labels: &labels_a,
        },
        NewChild {
            title: "B",
            body: "body B",
            labels: &labels_b,
        },
    ];

    let ids = store
        .split_bead(&BeadId::from("bf-parent"), &children)
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        ids,
        [BeadId::from("bf-child-a"), BeadId::from("bf-child-b")]
    );
    let invocations = fs::read_to_string(root.path().join("invocations.log")).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(invocations.matches("batch\n").count(), 1);
    let payload: serde_json::Value =
        serde_json::from_str(invocations.lines().nth(2).unwrap()).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(payload[2]["op"], "dep_add_blocker");
    assert_eq!(payload[2]["id"], "bf-parent");
    assert_eq!(payload[2]["blocker"], "@0");
    assert_eq!(payload[3]["op"], "dep_add_blocker");
    assert_eq!(payload[3]["id"], "bf-parent");
    assert_eq!(payload[3]["blocker"], "@1");
}

codex
The pre-consolidation history confirms the old batch function was removed when bead-forge moved to the descriptor-driven `CliBeadStore`. In the current code, `run_bf_update_batch` delegates to `run_argv`, and `run_argv` wraps every backend `Command::spawn()` with the ETXTBSY child wrapper; the wrapper also emits retry/success telemetry. I’ll verify the batch-facing tests and retry tests before recording this as already satisfied.

   154	    pub(super) async fn run_argv(
   155	        &self,
   156	        name: &str,
   157	        args: &[String],
   158	        timeout_secs: u64,
   159	    ) -> Result<String> {
   160	        let binary = self.binary.clone();
   161	        let workspace = self.workspace.clone();
   162	        let owned_args = args.to_vec();
   163	        let child = spawn_with_etxtbsy_retry_child(
   164	            || async {
   165	                let mut command = tokio::process::Command::new(&binary);
   166	                command
   167	                    .args(&owned_args)
   168	                    .current_dir(&workspace)
   169	                    .stdout(Stdio::piped())
   170	                    .stderr(Stdio::piped())

   741	
   742	impl CliBeadStore {
   743	    async fn claim_via_batch(&self, id: &BeadId, actor: &str) -> Result<ClaimResult> {
   744	        let shown = self.show(id).await?;
   745	        if shown.status != BeadStatus::Open {
   746	            return Ok(ClaimResult::NotClaimable {
   747	                reason: format!("bead is {}, not open", shown.status),
   748	            });
   749	        }
   750	        if let Some(claimed_by) = shown.assignee {
   751	            return Ok(ClaimResult::RaceLost { claimed_by });
   752	        }
   753	
   754	        // bf 0.4.1 removed --assignee from `update`, but its transactional
   755	        // batch update operation still accepts status and assignee together.
   756	        self.run_bf_update_batch(id, Some("in_progress"), Some(actor))
   757	            .await
   758	            .with_context(|| format!("bf batch update {id} (claim) failed"))?;
   759	
   760	        let claimed = self.show(id).await?;
   761	        if claimed.assignee.as_deref() == Some(actor) {
   762	            Ok(ClaimResult::Claimed(claimed))
   763	        } else {
   764	            Ok(ClaimResult::RaceLost {
   765	                claimed_by: claimed.assignee.unwrap_or_else(|| "(unknown)".to_string()),
   766	            })
   767	        }
   768	    }
   769	
   770	    async fn run_bf_update_batch(
   771	        &self,
   772	        id: &BeadId,
   773	        status: Option<&str>,
   774	        assignee: Option<&str>,
   775	    ) -> Result<()> {
   776	        let mut operation = serde_json::Map::new();
   777	        operation.insert("op".to_string(), serde_json::json!("update"));
   778	        operation.insert("id".to_string(), serde_json::json!(id.as_ref()));
   779	        if let Some(status) = status {
   780	            operation.insert("status".to_string(), serde_json::json!(status));
   781	        }
   782	        if let Some(assignee) = assignee {
   783	            operation.insert("assignee".to_string(), serde_json::json!(assignee));
   784	        }
   785	        let payload = serde_json::to_string(&vec![serde_json::Value::Object(operation)])?;
   786	        self.run_argv(
   787	            "batch_update",
   788	            &["batch".to_string(), "--json".to_string(), payload],
   789	            DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_SECS,
   790	        )
   791	        .await?;
   792	        Ok(())
   793	    }

   798	        match self.doctor_repair().await {

--- current wrapper telemetry ---
   440	pub async fn spawn_with_etxtbsy_retry<F, Fut, T>(
   441	    spawn_fn: F,
   442	    max_attempts: u32,
   443	    backoff_ms: u64,
   444	) -> std::io::Result<T>
   445	where
   446	    F: Fn() -> Fut,
   447	    Fut: std::future::Future<Output = std::io::Result<T>>,
   448	{
   449	    let mut last_err = None;
   450	    for attempt in 0..max_attempts {
   451	        match spawn_fn().await {
   452	            Ok(output) => {
   453	                if attempt > 0 {
   454	                    debug!(
   455	                        attempt = attempt + 1,
   456	                        max_attempts = max_attempts,
   457	                        function = "spawn_with_etxtbsy_retry",
   458	                        "ETXTBSY retry succeeded after {} attempts",
   459	                        attempt + 1
   460	                    );
   461	                }
   462	                return Ok(output);
   463	            }
   464	            Err(e) if e.raw_os_error() == Some(26) && attempt + 1 < max_attempts => {
   465	                last_err = Some(e);
   466	                warn!(
   467	                    attempt = attempt + 1,
   468	                    max_attempts = max_attempts,
   469	                    backoff_ms = backoff_ms,
   470	                    function = "spawn_with_etxtbsy_retry",
   471	                    "ETXTBSY error detected (attempt {}/{}), retrying in {}ms",
   472	                    attempt + 1,
   473	                    max_attempts,
   474	                    backoff_ms
   475	                );
   476	                tokio::time::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(backoff_ms)).await;
   477	            }
   478	            Err(e) => return Err(e),
   479	        }
   480	    }
   481	    Err(last_err.expect("loop always sets last_err before exhausting MAX_ATTEMPTS"))
   482	}
   483	
   484	/// Retry wrapper for `Command::spawn()` calls that handle ETXTBSY (errno 26).
   485	///
   486	/// Specialized version of `spawn_with_etxtbsy_retry` for subprocess spawns that
   487	/// return a `Child` process. Use this when you need to interact with the spawned
   488	/// process (e.g., for timeout handling with `kill_on_drop`).
   489	///
   490	/// # Parameters
   491	///
   492	/// * `spawn_fn` - An async function that attempts to spawn the subprocess
   493	/// * `max_attempts` - Maximum number of retry attempts (default: 5)
   494	/// * `backoff_ms` - Backoff delay between retries in milliseconds (default: 20)
   495	///
   496	/// # Returns
   497	///
   498	/// * `Ok(Child)` - The spawned child process on success
   499	/// * `Err(io::Error)` - The last error if all attempts are exhausted
   500	pub async fn spawn_with_etxtbsy_retry_child<F, Fut>(
   501	    spawn_fn: F,
   502	    max_attempts: u32,
   503	    backoff_ms: u64,
   504	) -> std::io::Result<tokio::process::Child>
   505	where
   506	    F: Fn() -> Fut,
   507	    Fut: std::future::Future<Output = std::io::Result<tokio::process::Child>>,
   508	{
   509	    spawn_with_etxtbsy_retry(spawn_fn, max_attempts, backoff_ms).await
   510	}
   511	
   512	/// Retry wrapper for subprocess spawns with exponential backoff for ETXTBSY (errno 26).

    /// Run `bf batch --json <ops>` and return stdout.
    ///
    /// The entire op array executes inside a single SQLite `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`
    /// transaction (bf `execute_batch`), so a crash or a failing op rolls the
    /// whole batch back. Used by [`BeadStore::split_bead`] for crash-safe mitosis.
    async fn run_bf_batch(&self, ops_json: &str) -> Result<String> {
        let timeout_duration = std::time::Duration::from_secs(30);
        let bf_path = self
            .resolve_bf()
            .map_err(|e| e.context("bf CLI not found; cannot run atomic batch"))?;

        let args = ["batch", "--json", ops_json];
        let bf_path_clone = bf_path.clone();
        let workspace = self.workspace.clone();
        let args = args.to_vec();
        let child = spawn_with_etxtbsy_retry_child(
            || async {
                let mut cmd = tokio::process::Command::new(&bf_path_clone);
                cmd.args(&args)
                    .current_dir(&workspace)
                    .stdout(std::process::Stdio::piped())
                    .stderr(std::process::Stdio::piped())
                    .kill_on_drop(true);
                cmd.spawn()
            },
            5,
            20,
        )
        .await
        .context("failed to spawn bf batch subprocess")?;

        let output = match tokio::time::timeout(timeout_duration, child.wait_with_output()).await {
            Ok(Ok(output)) => output,
            Ok(Err(e)) => return Err(e).context("bf batch subprocess failed"),
            Err(_) => {
                tracing::error!("bf batch subprocess timed out, killing process");
                bail!("bf batch subprocess timed out after 30s");
            }
        };

        let stdout =
            String::from_utf8(output.stdout).context("bf batch stdout was not valid UTF-8")?;

        if !output.status.success() {
            let code = output.status.code().unwrap_or(-1);
            let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
            bail!("bf batch exited with code {code}\nstderr: {stderr}");
        }

        Ok(stdout)
    }

Description: ## Deliverables

Update the `run_bf_batch` function (~line 864) to use the ETXTBSY retry wrapper instead of directly calling `cmd.spawn()`.

## Acceptance Criteria

- `run_bf_batch` uses the retry wrapper for all subprocess spawns
- Batch operations handle ETXTBSY errors gracefully
- No new panics from ETXTBSY during batch operations
- Tests pass with this change isolated
- Telemetry correctly reports retry attempts during batch ops

## Context

This is a medium-complexity spawn site (~line 864) that handles batch bf operations. Building on the pattern validated in the previous child bead.
